# Product guide updates

Every entry is written four ways (Pete, Oct 20, 2026): **for the practitioner** (plain language), **for the system administrator** (where and how to set it up), **for marketing** (positive), and **for FormedrixAI and partners** (the plain reality, including what is not built). Entries written before Batch 642 were rewritten into this form from their earlier text; the facts were carried over, and marketing and partner wording needs your review before it is used externally.

## Batch 755b (part 2): Eight more checklists in real words; unused demo checklists off the case list (Pete, Nov 1, 2026)

- **Practitioner:** The breast DCIS, lung adenocarcinoma, lung resection, kidney resection, skin melanoma, prostate resection, colorectal local excision and colorectal further investigations checklists read in plain clinical words. When you add a checklist to a case, five demonstration checklists no longer appear in the list.
- **System administrator:** Nothing to set up. The five hidden checklists (breast surgical excision, kidney biopsy, prostate TURP or enucleation, Wilms tumor resection and biopsy) stay in the Synoptic Library with the Demo wording tag.
- **Marketing:** Fourteen of PathScribe's checklists now read in plain clinical words, written by PathScribe.
- **FormedrixAI/partners:** Built and tested. All are still "Demo wording" until a pathologist reviews them. The five hidden checklists keep placeholder wording. Real names in the demo data (hospitals, some staff and surgeons) are awaiting Pete's decision.
- **User Guide / Admin Guide:** none. **Training:** none.

## Batch 755b (part 1): Breast and UK checklists in real words; the Manchester demo pathologist is fictional (Pete, Nov 1, 2026)

- **Practitioner:** The breast invasive carcinoma checklist and the two UK-layout checklists (colorectal resection and prostate biopsy) read in plain clinical words, with follow-up questions shown only when they apply (for example the involved margins only when a margin is involved). The Manchester demonstration cases' reports read in real words.
- **System administrator:** Nothing to set up. A UK demonstration signs in as Dr. Imogen Hartley (imogen.hartley@demo.pathscribe.ai, the shared demo password), the fictional Manchester pathologist. Paul Carter, who advises on UK workflow, has his own sign-in without clinical credentials.
- **Marketing:** PathScribe's breast and UK checklists are written by PathScribe in the terms UK and US pathologists use every day.
- **FormedrixAI/partners:** Built and checked in the browser. Still "Demo wording" until a pathologist reviews them. Not done yet (Batch 755b continues): breast DCIS, lung adenocarcinoma, the other six checklists with demo cases, and hiding the five unused ones from the picker. The Manchester demo still names real hospitals (Wythenshawe Hospital, Manchester Royal Infirmary, North Manchester General) and the trust: Pete to decide. Other demo staff carry clinical credentials under what may be real people's names (see the batch notes).
- **User Guide / Admin Guide:** none. **Training:** UK demonstrations sign in as Dr. Imogen Hartley.

## Batch 755a: Plain checklist names, a "Demo wording" tag, and no copies of demo wording (Pete, Oct 31, 2026)

- **Practitioner:** The demonstration checklists now have plain names ("Colon resection", "Prostate needle biopsy", "Radical prostatectomy") with a small grey **Demo wording** tag beside the name. The tag means a pathologist has not yet reviewed the wording. These checklists can't be copied: to build your own, start from scratch, from one of PathScribe's own checklists, or from a protocol your laboratory is licensed for. The autopsy demo cases open on PathScribe's autopsy gross examination checklist.
- **System administrator:** Configuration → Synoptic Library: a tagged checklist has no **Duplicate** or **New Version**, and **Build / Customise → existing template** lists only checklists that may be copied, with a note saying why the others are missing. Opening a copy by address shows "This checklist can't be copied". The service refuses the save as well. No setting; the tag comes off a checklist only when ForMedrixAI records a pathologist's review of its wording (docs/architecture/DEMO_CHECKLISTS.md).
- **Marketing:** Every checklist PathScribe ships says plainly where its wording stands, and nothing unreviewed can slip into a laboratory's own library.
- **FormedrixAI/partners:** Built and checked in the browser. The tag also governs copying and customer builds (one flag). Not done: 16 checklists still carry placeholder answers (Batch 755b, breast invasive first); copies made before this batch keep the inherited wording untagged; the tag is not shown in every place a checklist name appears (the Sequencer, the pre-finalisation window and the AI report view show the name without it).
- **User Guide:** the synoptic chapter's checklist names and a line on the tag. **Admin Guide:** chapter 6 (Synoptic Library) updated in six languages.
- **Training:** point at the tag when opening a checklist in a demo; say a pathologist reviews the wording before it is offered for copying.
- **Standards:** supports content provenance for CAP/ISO 15189 document control (who approved a protocol's wording).

## Batch 754: The demonstration checklists never reach a customer (Pete, Oct 31, 2026)

- **Practitioner:** Nothing changes on the demonstration site. At a customer site the 19 "Generic Template" checklists are not there; the lab uses its own checklists and PathScribe's own (Mohs, oral cavity, neck dissection, cytology, grossing, autopsy).
- **System administrator:** A customer build (`npm run build:server`) leaves the 19 out, and its check fails the build if one is in it. For an organisation on a deployment that has them, ForMedrixAI support opens Configuration → Synoptic Library → All protocols → **Demonstration checklists**, checks what would be removed, and removes them (each is archived: not offered for new reports; a report already made with one still opens; audited). Against an API server: `scripts/remove-demo-checklists.mjs` (dry run unless `--apply`; Planned until the server offers the endpoint). Steps: `docs/architecture/DEMO_CHECKLISTS.md`.
- **Marketing:** Every customer starts with a clean synoptic library: PathScribe's own checklists and the content the lab is licensed for.
- **FormedrixAI/partners:** Built and checked: the customer build really drops the 19 files (built with `--mode server` and inspected), the removal in the browser (dry run, 19 archived, audit). Not built: the API endpoint the script calls; blocking an administrator from restoring an archived demonstration checklist; and copies a customer makes of these checklists keep the inherited question list, so copying them and removing the originals before go-live does not remove the risk (see `DEMO_CHECKLISTS.md`). The demo cases inside the mock services still name the checklists until the HTTP services replace the mocks (PS-539). Also deleted: an old, unused protocol editor whose registry was labelled "CAP …". Non-English text is machine translated.
- **User Guide:** none. **Admin Guide:** none (the card is for ForMedrixAI support only; a support runbook entry is the right home).
- **Training:** none.
- **Standards:** none claimed.

## Batch 753: The prostate checklists read in real words (seed row S15, Video 9) (Pete, Oct 31, 2026)

- **Practitioner:** The prostate needle biopsy and radical prostatectomy checklists now use plain clinical wording instead of "Option 1", "Option 2". Questions that only matter sometimes appear only then: on a prostatectomy, the margin details show once a margin is positive, and the extension details once extraprostatic extension is present.
- **System administrator:** Nothing to set up. A browser that used the demo before needs one Full Reset to load the corrected demo cases (S26-4404, S26-5407).
- **Marketing:** PathScribe's prostate checklists speak the pathologist's language, from Grade Group to margins, and stay in step with the organ map they draw.
- **FormedrixAI/partners:** The wording is PathScribe's own, written on standard nomenclature (ISUP Grade Groups, WHO tumour types, TNM codes); no CAP or RCPath text was copied (Pete's condition). The question set was inherited from the earlier, licence-cleaned templates, so counsel should confirm the position before the checklists are offered as content; they are not CAP- or RCPath-endorsed. Demo data corrected: S26-4404's summary answers disagreed with its map (12 cores and 3+3 against six cores and 3+4) and now agree; its demo amendment shows only the four corrected answers; S26-5407's whole checklist is answered; MFT26-8804's AI suggestions agree with its map. 16 other generic checklists still show placeholders, and three older demo cases (S26-4412, MFT26-8802-PR-BX, MPA26-1003-PRO) keep placeholder-style words in their descriptions. Checked with automated tests and in the browser (every checklist tab of S26-4404 and S26-5407: no placeholder on screen).
- **User Guide:** none (the guide does not quote checklist answers). **Admin Guide:** none.
- **Training:** Video 9 steps 1 to 8 can be recorded; run Full Reset before each take.
- **Standards:** none claimed.

## Batch 752: Slide images hosted for the demo, and the handover note brought up to date (Pete, Oct 31, 2026)

- **Practitioner:** Nothing changes on screen yet. Once the security policy change below is made, the demo's slides S26-5404-B1-L1, C1-L1 and C1-L2 show real lymph node previews, and Open slide shows the slide on the demonstration page.
- **System administrator:** The demo's images are in the Cloudflare R2 bucket `pathscribe-demo-slides`, public at `https://slides-demo.formedrix.ai`. Vercel (Production) holds `VITE_SLIDE_VIEWER_URL_TEMPLATE` and `VITE_SLIDE_THUMBNAIL_URL_TEMPLATE`; they survive every deploy, need a redeploy when changed, and must be added for Preview if preview builds should show slides. `scripts/Setup-PathScribeSlideDemo.ps1` rebuilds and uploads the images (it asks for an R2 token's keys and stores none). Steps: `SLIDE_IMAGE_DEMO.md`.
- **Marketing:** The demo now shows real, openly licensed lymph node slides opening beside PathScribe, as a lab's own digital pathology application would.
- **FormedrixAI/partners:** The previews stay blank on the demo until `https://slides-demo.formedrix.ai` is added to `img-src` (`deploy/security-headers/`, then regenerate `vercel.json`); that folder is not in Claude's working copy. Three of the five planned slides are hosted; the two breast slides wait for an image whose licence has been read. The script was written for Windows and run by Pete; Claude could not run PowerShell. The handover note (`docs/developer/HANDOVER_FIRST_RELEASE.md`) now lists Batches 699 to 751 and the new open items; its Jira counts are still those of Oct 26.
- **User Guide:** none. **Admin Guide:** none (the Slide images settings are unchanged).
- **Training:** none yet; Video 7 steps 17 and 18 use these images once the previews show.
- **Standards:** none claimed.

## Batch 751: The organ map on Assist cases, and delivering it to the laboratory system (seed row S15) (Pete, Oct 31, 2026)

- **Practitioner:** On an Assist case your laboratory system writes the report, so the organ map is there to help you fill in the checklist: it is drawn from your answers and clicking it takes you to the question, but there is no Insert into report. The panel says "Your laboratory system writes this report." If your laboratory has turned on map delivery, a quiet line tells you when the map file was sent; you never have to do anything, and a problem with the delivery never stops you finalising.
- **System administrator:** Facility editor → LIS Integration (performing lab) → **Organ map delivery**: Off (default), Text only, or Text and image file, with the file format (PNG or PDF) and the destination (a folder, an SFTP address or an interface engine channel; no user name or password). Saved on its own with the new permission `config:organ-map-delivery:manage` (Admin and Lab Director); every change, every delivery and every failure is audited. Failed deliveries appear in the outbound queue with retry, which draws the file again from the final answers. Configuration → System → Organ Maps still links each checklist to its map.
- **Marketing:** The anatomical map works for every lab, including labs whose own system writes the report: the map can go to that system as text and a picture, with no extra step for the pathologist.
- **FormedrixAI/partners:** Phase 1 (no map in an Assist report, Mohs included) and phase 2 (delivery) are built in the browser and tested; the file is drawn in the browser at finalising (PNG, or a one-page PDF holding a JPEG), so a release at the end of a release buffer with no page open queues the file as held until someone retries it. The API server must draw it itself (Planned). The result message already carried documents and document references, so no format change was needed (spec 12.8(a), answered). Real-site file formats are to be settled with the first customer; the regulatory adviser is to confirm the wording. The map is a report figure drawn from entered answers, not a medical image; no AI. The demo's Video 9 cases S26-4404 and S26-4463 became Orchestrator cases; the prostate checklists still carry placeholder answers outside the map section.
- **User Guide:** Report: Synoptic reporting (Organ map and Mohs stages). **Admin Guide:** Facility editor, LIS Integration tab (six languages, published).
- **Training:** open S26-5405 as Dr. Sarah Johnson and press Map; then, as the administrator, the Desert Valley facility's LIS Integration tab, and the failed map file of S26-5406 in the outbound queue.
- **Standards:** none claimed.

## Batch 750: Slide images: a reference preview and opening the lab's own digital pathology application (seed row S10) (Pete, Oct 31, 2026)

- **Practitioner:** Each scanned slide in the block and stain window (and on a cytology case screened on whole-slide images) shows a small preview, marked "Reference preview. Not for diagnosis." Select it and the slide opens in your laboratory's own digital pathology application, in its own window that remembers where you put it. A slide without an image says where it is: No image yet, Queued, Scanning, Scan failed or Failed image quality. To choose where the slide window opens, use the workstation menu: **Slide image window → Set position…**, move the window, then **Save position**.
- **System administrator:** Configuration → System → Lab Materials & Workflows → Vendor Integrations → WSI Viewers → **Slide images**: the viewer address (opens the slide in your digital pathology application) and the preview image address (from your scanner or image system), each an https template with `{{slideId}}` and, if wanted, `{{accession}}`; a facility with its own system gets its own pair. Blank uses the deployment's addresses (`VITE_SLIDE_VIEWER_URL_TEMPLATE`, `VITE_SLIDE_THUMBNAIL_URL_TEMPLATE`, also in Runtime settings). Saving needs `config:vendor-integrations:manage`. Opening a slide needs the new `case:slide-image:open` (seeded for every built-in role with case access, as report images are) and is written to the audit log (who, which slide, when; no patient name). Add the preview host to the site's security policy (img-src), or previews will not load.
- **Marketing:** PathScribe works beside the digital pathology system the lab already has: a glance at the slide, one select to open it where it belongs.
- **FormedrixAI/partners:** PathScribe has no slide viewer and does no image processing; the preview is shown as received. Built: the settings, the image states (from the scanner unload, now recorded on the slide), the open action with its capability and audit, the remembered window with a Save position page, and moving a saved window to the main screen when its screen has gone (Chrome and Edge, once the browser's window placement permission is given; other browsers reopen at the saved coordinates). Tested in headless Chromium only, on one virtual screen: the multi-monitor behaviour has not been seen on real hardware. Previews delivered by the interface engine are Planned (the service and its API contract exist; nothing sends them yet). The security policy's img-src is not yet generated from the preview address. The older WSI Viewers list still works as a fallback for the viewer address. Demo: five openly licensed images and a standalone demonstration page (tools/demo-slide-viewer) are prepared outside PathScribe (docs/product/SLIDE_IMAGE_DEMO.md); until they are hosted and the addresses set, demo slides show their states and "No viewer address is set." Keep clinical-use claims out of all material: the FDA question is open.
- **User Guide:** Slide Images (help:wsi-viewer, rewritten), Cytology Screening Case, Report: microscopic and material, WSI Scan. **Admin Guide:** Digital pathology and Vendor Integrations (WSI Viewers, Slide images).
- **Training:** open S26-5404 as Dr. Sarah Johnson, Edit a block, select a preview; show S26-5403 (Queued, Scan failed) and S26-5404 C1-L2 (Failed image quality); set the window position from the workstation menu.
- **Standards:** none claimed.

## Batch 743: On an Assist case, attachments are references only (Pete, Oct 30, 2026)

- **Practitioner:** On an Assist case (your LIS writes the report), the Attachments window is for references: attach or link outside consult notes, earlier reports and scanned histories, open them and the case's photos while you work, and remove them from the case. There are no tick boxes, sections or **Insert into Report**, and the window says why: "On an Assist case your LIS writes the report. Attachments here are references for this case, to open while you work; nothing here is added to the LIS report."
- **System administrator:** Nothing to set: the case's reporting mode decides. The same permissions apply (`case:attachment:upload`, `case:attachment:link`, `case:attachment:unlink`). In Assist the primary LIS stays the custodian of the files; PathScribe keeps pointers and an audit trail.
- **Marketing:** In Assist mode PathScribe keeps the outside notes and images for a case at the pathologist's fingertips while the LIS stays the system of record.
- **FormedrixAI/partners:** Built in the demo build, checked with automated tests and in the browser (S26-4680, an Assist case: note shown, no tick boxes, sections or Insert; O26-0001, an Orchestrator case: unchanged). The service refuses placing in a report on an Assist case (`assistCase`), and still lets an older placement be taken out. **Corrected in this batch:** Batch 742's note that an Assist case's report-page PDF had a missing body was wrong; in Assist the LIS writes the report and PathScribe sends the synoptic workups back, so that PDF is only PathScribe's own snapshot. **Planned (Pete's dev note):** the LIS pushes an Assist case's attachment URLs through the interface engine, received on the existing LIS ingestion layer and listed as "From the LIS" (`docs/architecture/CASE_ATTACHMENTS_API.md` §6a). **Left out for now (Pete):** sending images to the LIS with the workups. AI reading attachments is not built. Non-English text is machine translated.
- **Standards:** supports keeping the LIS as the single record holder (CAP, ISO 15189 document control). No certification claimed.

## Batch 742: Case attachments, with images printed inside their report section (Pete's "Case Image & File Attachments, Release 1", Oct 30, 2026)

- **Practitioner:** The report page's action bar (and the Cytology Workspace) shows **Attachments (n)**, or **Add Attachments** when the case has none. It opens one window with the case's gross and block-face photos, its intraoperative and consultation photos, slide regions (once the slide viewer exists) and any files or links attached. **Attach External File…** opens your computer's own file picker (local folders and mapped network drives): PDF, JPG or PNG; a Word document must be saved as PDF first. A file over 25 MB asks before it is sent. Tick what belongs in the report, add a caption if you like, choose the section (the one you are working in is chosen for you) and **Insert into Report**: an image prints right under that section, an attached PDF is named there and its pages follow the report. The report preview shows the same. A signed report changes only through an amendment or addendum. **Remove from case** unlinks a file; it stays in the hospital's storage.
- **System administrator:** Three new permissions, given to every role with case access, as `report:case-images:attach` is: `case:attachment:upload`, `case:attachment:link`, `case:attachment:unlink`. Attached files go to the hospital's own storage through the **storage adapter**, set up per deployment outside PathScribe (a pre-signed upload to a cloud bucket or VNA, a network share through a background worker, or the interface engine); PathScribe keeps only the pointer it gets back, and the adapter does the malware scanning, retention and legal hold (`docs/architecture/CASE_ATTACHMENTS_API.md`). The adapter can change the 25 MB warning and set a hard limit. **Redeploy `functions/`**: the report renderer now draws images inside their sections. Audit: "Case Attachment Uploaded", "Case Attachment Linked", "Case Attachment Unlinked", "Report Image Linked" (never the file name or caption).
- **Marketing:** Every photo and document for a case in one place, and images exactly where the pathologist wants them in the report: under the gross description, beside the diagnosis. Files stay in the hospital's own storage, wherever that is: cloud, network share or interface engine.
- **FormedrixAI/partners:** Built in the demo build, checked with automated tests and in the browser: on S26-4680 (three seeded demo illustrations, a Word file refused, a PDF attached, items placed, the badge counting 3 then 4, the window locked once signed) and on O26-0001 (an attached picture placed under Gross Description, printed there by the updated renderer and shown there in the preview). The demo's storage keeps an attached file in the browser tab only (it is gone after a reload; the report then prints Image Unavailable). Not built: the storage adapter and its three endpoints on the API server, server-side fetching when the report is made, slide regions (they come with the slide viewer), images inside sections of the **cytology** report (they still print after its pages), Word conversion (not planned for Release 1). The Batch 609 Attach images window is replaced. **Correction (same batch, after Pete's review):** an earlier version of this entry called the body-less PDF of an Assist case a fault. It isn't: in Assist mode the LIS writes the report, and PathScribe sends each synoptic workup back (its structured result, its own workup PDF and a text block, `services/reports/dispatchAssistSynopticResult.ts`); the report page's PDF of an Assist case is only PathScribe's own version snapshot. For Assist cases see Batch 743: the window keeps references only, and sending images to the LIS is left out for now (Pete). Non-English text is machine translated.
- **Standards:** supports HIPAA and GDPR data minimisation (no stored files; file names kept out of the audit log) and CAP report-content practice for images. No certification claimed.

## Batch 741: Divide specimen, with the volume accounted for, and aliquot tracking to Result back (Pete's fluid decisions 2 and 3, Oct 30, 2026)

- **Practitioner:** On the Grossing screen and at the Cytology Preparation bench, **Divide specimen** splits a specimen into aliquots for flow cytometry, microbiology, molecular or other tests. Enter the starting volume and each aliquot's volume and destination (a fluid or FNA comes with its usual rows); PathScribe shows how much is split and how much remains, and refuses a split larger than the start. Each aliquot then moves Not sent → Sent → Received → Result back; Result back asks for the receiving department's accession number.
- **System administrator:** Two permissions: `material:specimen:divide` (roles with Grossing or Cytology Preparation) and `material:aliquot-status:update` (Cytology Preparation or Batch Management). Processing Protocols: a protocol can suggest its division (the fluid and FNA demo protocols suggest flow cytometry, microbiology and molecular). Every division and status change is audited ("Specimen Divided", "Aliquot Status Changed").
- **Marketing:** Every millilitre accounted for: split a fluid for flow, micro and molecular in seconds, and follow each portion until its result is back.
- **FormedrixAI/partners:** Built in the demo build, checked with automated tests and in the browser (S26-5005 at the Cytology Preparation bench: 5 mL of 10 mL divided into two aliquots, one moved to Result back after the receiving accession was asked for). Not on Accession (the case's material exists only once it is accessioned; dividing happens at Grossing or the preparation bench). PathScribe tracks the aliquot, never its result; an aliquot leaving the building rides an External Referral batch as any other material, and completing that batch does not yet move the aliquot to Sent. Destinations are typed (department or reference laboratory), not picked from a list. The API server must apply the same rules (`services/material/specimenDivision.ts`). Non-English text is machine translated.
- **Standards:** supports specimen accountability and traceability (CAP, ISO 15189). No certification claimed.

## Batch 740: The Cytology Preparation bench, with a scanned hand-off of every cell block to histology (Pete's fluid decision 1, Oct 30, 2026)

- **Practitioner:** Fluids are now prepared at their own bench: Pathology Workspace → **Cytology Preparation**. Scan the fluid, record volume, appearance and yield, accept the suggested slides and print them, make a cell block (**Make a cell block from this decant**, **Print label**). A cell block goes to histology only after **Hand off**: scan the fluid or aliquot it came from, then its cassette. The Microtome now takes only blocks and handed-off cell blocks; a fluid scanned there is sent back to the preparation bench.
- **System administrator:** New screen permission `screen:cytology-prep:open` (Histotechnologist and Cytotechnologist) and action permission `cytology:cell-block:hand-off` (whoever opens the bench). New station stage **Cytology Preparation**; the demo has Cytology Preparation — Bench 1 (CYTOPREP-01), printing through the seeded printer. Every hand-off is audited ("Cell Block Handed Off To Histology").
- **Marketing:** Cytology and histology each work on their own screen, and no cell block reaches the microtome without a scanned chain of custody back to its source fluid.
- **FormedrixAI/partners:** Built in the demo build, checked with automated tests and in the browser (S26-5005: a cell block made, a wrong source refused, the hand-off recorded; the Microtome refusing the fluid and taking the handed-off cell block). The bench reuses the Microtome's logic and panels; its foot pedal, comments and billing panel are not on it yet. Cell blocks planned at Accession by a protocol are refused at the Microtome until handed off (by design; the seeded cell block on S26-5005 is handed off). The cassette label printed here is the decant container label. Video 8 needs rewording: steps 3 to 7 at the Cytology Preparation bench, and a hand-off before step 11. Non-English text is machine translated.
- **Standards:** supports specimen traceability and chain of custody (CAP, ISO 15189). No certification claimed.

## Batch 739: Aliquots, ThinPrep and SurePath alike, and cytospins for small fluids (Pete's fluid decisions 4, 5 and 6, Oct 30, 2026)

- **Practitioner:** A portion of a specimen set aside for a test is now an **aliquot** and says what it is for ("D1 — Aliquot for HPV testing"), instead of being called a cell block. A ThinPrep or a SurePath Pap without HPV now arrives with its vial and its Pap slide, ready for the processing batch. The batch step is now called **Liquid-Based Cytology Processing**, and when you make one you choose its **Processor** (the first is chosen for you). At the Microtome, a CSF or any fluid of 5 mL or less is suggested two cytospin slides instead of a liquid-based slide and a smear.
- **System administrator:** Equipment has a new kind, **Liquid-based cytology processor**; the demo has a ThinPrep and a SurePath processor. Processing Protocols: a decant track can make a cell block, a residual fluid vial or an aliquot with a purpose (HPV, molecular, flow cytometry, microbiology). New protocols for the ThinPrep and SurePath Pap and a SurePath Pap stain (billing code PAP-SUREPATH). No new permission.
- **Marketing:** Vendor-neutral liquid-based cytology: ThinPrep and SurePath run side by side, every aliquot is labelled for what it is, and scarce fluids get the preparation that makes the most of them.
- **FormedrixAI/partners:** Built in the demo build, checked with automated tests and in the browser (the renamed step and the Processor field in New Container). The instrument order now names the chosen processor (code, make, model) instead of a fixed "Hologic"; the interface engine and the API server must accept the new payload (`INTERFACE_TRANSACTIONS.md`) and run the HPV-aliquot data migration (`CASE_WORKFLOWS_API.md`). Cytospin stays a slide's preparation method, with no batch step (decision 5). Still to build from the same decisions: the Cytology Preparation bench with its hand-off scan (decision 1), and Divide specimen with volumes, destinations and statuses (decisions 2 and 3). Video 8 step 8 should say "Liquid-Based Cytology Processing". Non-English text is machine translated.
- **Standards:** supports traceability of every portion of a specimen (CAP, ISO 15189). No certification claimed.

## Batch 738: Demo seed data for the videos, part 1 (S1, S2, S3, S12, S13), and the review window shows answers in words (Pete, Oct 30, 2026)

- **Practitioner:** The "Review before finalising" window now shows each synoptic question and answer in words ("Procedure: Sigmoidectomy"), not stored codes. On Accession, the received time defaults to your local time (it could be an hour or more off in the evening). A colon, breast, skin, small biopsy or prostate core specimen now arrives at Grossing with its blocks laid out from a protocol, so Complete grossing can be reached. The colon synoptic reads in plain pathology terms (procedure, site, type, extent, invasion, nodes, pTNM), and questions that don't apply are hidden (rectal location for a colon tumour, involved margins when margins are free).
- **System administrator:** Seven grossing protocols are seeded in Configuration → Processing Protocols and linked to their Specimen Dictionary entries (editable). In the demo every histology bench prints through the one seeded printer profile (PathScribe agent, answered by a labelled Simulated result), and Print Settings carries a GS1 internal-use GTIN for the demo only. Demo users: Jordan Ellis (administrator only); Connor Whitlock and Kevin Brooks have home benches.
- **Marketing:** From order to signed report in one take: the demo carries a complete colon cancer case, real-world orders and benches that print.
- **FormedrixAI/partners:** Built in the demo build, checked with automated tests and in the browser (sign-out of S26-4680 to Pending Release, the review window, a simulated cassette reprint at Embedding, Accession's received time). The colon wording is PathScribe's own, not CAP text; 18 other generic templates still show placeholder options. The demo GTIN is a GS1 restricted-circulation number, not ForMedrixAI's. Not built: an intake type on orders; billing charge records for S26-4680. Found and not fixed: sign-out waits for the AI critical-findings check with no time limit; the report page's "required fields incomplete" banner shows on every synoptic report. Status of every seed row: `docs/product/DEMO_SEED_DATA.md`.
- **Standards:** supports fixation-time documentation (CAP/ASCO breast fixation), GS1 identification (restricted circulation for internal use). No certification claimed.

## Batch 737: Add-on orders on a matrix block, the megablock cassette label, and Batch Management checks for exhausted blocks and large-format slides (Pete, Oct 30, 2026)

- **Practitioner:** On Add-On Orders, a case with a shared (matrix) block lists it under **Shared (matrix) blocks**; tick it to order for the whole block, or tick only the parts you want (listed as pieces, for example "Piece 1 (D)"). If the tissue you chose is used up, the order is refused with a message saying so. At the Microtome you can now mark one part of a matrix block used up. Megablock cassettes print on the megablock cassette label. In Batch Management, scanning an exhausted block into a batch still adds it, with a warning that stays until you close it; a large-format slide (from a megablock) is refused by a staining rack that does not take large-format slides, with a message naming the rack.
- **System administrator:** Configuration → System → **Equipment** now lists the lab's reusable staining racks with **Takes large-format slides** (saved at once; `config:equipment:manage`, Admin and Lab Director). In **New Container**, a Staining carrier has **Takes large-format slides (megablocks)**. The printers need a megablock cassette template (`ZPL-CASSETTE-MEGA-V1`) set up by the interface team. Every exhausted block scanned into a batch is in the audit log ("Exhausted Block Scanned Into Batch").
- **Marketing:** Shared cassettes and oversized sections get the same care as every other block: order a stain for just the part you need, print the right cassette label, and never stain a large-format slide in the wrong rack.
- **FormedrixAI/partners:** Built in the demo build, checked with automated tests and in the browser (Equipment staining racks, the matrix block and its parts on Add-On Orders for S26-4404, the large-format box in New Container). An order on some parts of a matrix block is one slide cut through the whole block, marked with the parts it is for (`targetSpecimenIds`); PathScribe cannot cut only some parts. A matrix block cannot be re-embedded (as before), so an order on its used-up tissue is refused rather than offered alternatives. Rack registration has no add or retire screen yet (only the large-format setting). Lab-configurable block types are not built. Non-English text is machine translated.
- **Standards:** supports traceability of shared and large-format material (for example CAP and ISO 15189). No certification claimed.

## Batch 736: Bench reasons in the Reason Dictionary, with their codes kept; header search above the keyboard (Pete, Oct 30, 2026)

- **Practitioner:** The reasons you tap at the bench (Block exhausted at the Microtome; Abort, complete with exceptions and cold chain in Batch Management; an order or answer on an exhausted block on Add-On Orders) are now your lab's own wording. Tap one; it stays highlighted; tap it again to clear it. The box underneath is a note: optional, added after the reason. With no reason tapped you can still type one. The search box at the top is a typing box, so it opens the keyboard; its results now stay above the keyboard and scroll.
- **System administrator:** Configuration → System → Administration & Compliance → **Bench Reasons** (new; `config:bench-reasons:manage`, Admin and Lab Director). Five groups. **Reword** a reason (a default reason shows in each user's language until reworded, then as typed), add your own, put them in order (↑ / ↓), retire or restore, or go back to the default wording. Each reason has a **Code** that never changes; records and audit entries keep the code beside the words, and the Reconciliation exceptions report has a **Reason code** column, so counts stay consistent after rewording. 18 defaults ship in six languages.
- **Marketing:** Every lab speaks its own language at the bench, and every reason is still counted the same way in reports and audits.
- **FormedrixAI/partners:** Built in the demo build, checked with automated tests and in the browser. One list for the whole organisation (no per-facility wording yet). A reworded reason is shown as typed in every language. The codes are stored on the add-on exception, the batch abort, override and cold-chain records and in the audit detail; the API server must store and return them (`LAB_DICTIONARIES_API.md` §7, `CASE_WORKFLOWS_API.md`). The Batch 735 preset list (`quickReasonSets.ts`) is deleted (`DELETED_FILES.txt`). Non-English text is machine translated.
- **Standards:** supports consistent, auditable reason coding (for example ISO 15189 records, CAP documentation of exceptions); WCAG 2.2 target size for the search results' close button. No certification claimed.

## Batch 735: Station screens work by touch (Station Touch Specification v1, Pete, Oct 30, 2026)

- **Practitioner:** On a bench tablet the Grossing, Embedding, Microtome, Slide Distribution, Add-On Order and Batch Management screens now have finger-sized buttons, chips and tick boxes, bigger main actions and 16 px text in fields. Scan fields take a barcode without opening the on-screen keyboard and stay ready for the next scan; **⌨ Type** opens the keyboard when you need to type an ID. Required reasons are one tap (Block exhausted at the Microtome, Abort and complete-with-exceptions in Batch Management, answering a Block exhausted flag on Add-On Orders), with a note optional. At the Microtome, Batch print order uses **↑** and **↓** instead of dragging. When the keyboard is up, the field stays in view and, if the form's Save button would be hidden, a copy of it sits just above the keyboard. Tap a label with extra information to read it.
- **System administrator:** Nothing to set up: the touch layout switches on by itself on a touch device. Per computer, the station menu at the top has **Touch layout on this computer**: **Auto** (default), **Always on**, **Always off** (a bench desktop can keep the compact layout; a touch laptop can be forced on). It is stored on the computer, like its station. No new permission.
- **Marketing:** PathScribe's bench screens are built for gloved hands and barcode scanners: 44 px targets, no keyboard popping up on every scan, and one-tap reasons, while desktops keep the compact view.
- **FormedrixAI/partners:** Built in the demo build, checked with automated tests and re-audited in desktop Chromium at 1280 × 800 and 1024 × 768 (touch on, off and Auto, and Auto on an emulated tablet). Not yet confirmed on a real iPad with a keyboard-wedge scanner and gloves, which the spec requires before any material calls a screen touch-ready (`docs/product/STATION_TOUCH.md`). The preset reason lists are fixed in the code (not yet in the Reason Dictionary). The header search box still opens the keyboard (it is typed into as often as scanned). Below 1280 px the header shows the logo mark only and hides the signed-in name (the avatar stays). Non-English text is machine translated.
- **Standards:** supports WCAG 2.2 success criteria 2.5.5 (target size, enhanced), 2.5.7 (dragging movements) and 2.5.8 (target size, minimum). No certification claimed.

## Batch 734: Ad-hoc carriers move without asking, and direct printing from the browser is off unless a facility allows it (Pete, Oct 30, 2026)

- **Practitioner:** Scan an open ad-hoc carrier at your bench and it simply moves to your station, with a short note saying where it came from; you are asked only when the step does not fit the carrier. If a label will not print and the message says direct printing is off for this facility, print through the interface engine or the browser print dialog, or ask your administrator.
- **System administrator:** Facility Dictionary → a performing lab → **Printing** tab → **Allow direct printing from the browser** (off by default; needs the new permission `config:direct-device-printing:manage`, granted to Admin and Lab Director). It covers QZ Tray, the PathScribe agent, Zebra Browser Print and BarTender. Every direct print, and every refused one, is in the audit log ("Direct Device Print", "Direct Device Print Blocked"). The demo always allows direct printing. Printer addresses stay on the printer profiles.
- **Marketing:** Benches keep moving: ad-hoc boxes follow the tech without extra taps, and hospitals decide, site by site, whether browsers may talk to printers directly, with every print on the record.
- **FormedrixAI/partners:** Built in the demo build and checked with automated tests. The API server must enforce the same switch on its own print routes, and must refuse direct calls it cannot see (a browser bridge prints without the server). A refused print is not retried; the job is marked failed with a reason. Non-English text is machine translated.
- **Standards:** supports change control and audit of device access (for example HIPAA security rule audit controls, ISO 15189). No certification claimed.

## Batch 733: One flag per exhausted block, and destructive cutting only while tissue may remain (Pete, Oct 30, 2026)

- **Practitioner:** At the Microtome, one **Block exhausted** flag now covers every slide on the block still waiting to be cut, and each ordering pathologist gets one e-mail. On Add-On Orders one answer applies to all of those slides; tick **Answer this slide only** when one slide needs a different choice. **Approve Destructive Cutting** is offered only while some tissue may still be left; it needs a reason and warns that no later recut, biomarker or molecular test will be possible from the block. If the block was already exhausted, it is not offered.
- **System administrator:** Nothing to set up. Permissions are unchanged (`add-on:exception:flag`, `add-on:order:place`, `add-on:exception:approve-destructive-cut`, `material:reembed:request`).
- **Marketing:** A four-stain panel on a used-up block takes one flag and one decision, not four, and PathScribe protects small biopsies from being cut away without a recorded reason.
- **FormedrixAI/partners:** Built in the demo build and checked with automated tests. A group answer with "use an unstained slide" picks the block's free unstained slides in order; the pathologist cannot choose which slide each order gets except by answering slide by slide. Non-English text is machine translated.
- **Standards:** supports documented decisions on tissue use (for example CAP and ISO 15189 traceability). No certification claimed.

## Batch 732: Megablocks and multi-piece blocks

- **Practitioner:** At grossing, **Type & pieces** under a block sets its type (standard, megablock or multi-piece) and its pieces: the part each came from, a description, orientation, ink and position in the block. A megablock's slides are large format and print on the large-format label. On Add-On Orders you can tick the pieces an order is for (the core with the suspicious tissue, say), and order a range of levels with several levels to a slide. The Embedding, Microtome and Slide Distribution screens show the block type, the slide size and the pieces; at the Microtome **Piece exhausted** marks one piece used up while the others remain, and an order for a used-up piece gets the same alternatives as an exhausted block, including another piece that remains.
- **System administrator:** Configuration → System → Equipment: **Takes large-format slides** for stainers and slide scanners (Slide Distribution sends a large-format slide only to a scanner with it ticked). Marking a piece used up needs `material:end-state:record`. The label templates `ZPL-SLIDE-LF-V1` (large-format slide) and `ZPL-CASSETTE-MEGA-V1` are named to the printer through the interface engine; they must exist on the printer side.
- **Marketing:** Prostate cores, whole-mount sections and megablocks are tracked piece by piece with one barcode, so the pathologist orders exactly the core they need.
- **Review fixes (independent code review of Batches 731–732, applied here):** on an exhausted block every slide still to be cut is now held until the pathologist answers (before, only the flagged slide was held); the Microtome's flag goes on a slide still waiting to be cut, never a finished or cancelled one; a recut can no longer be answered with an existing slide; an alternative needs a response note; a moved order takes its control slide; only unstained slides still at hand are offered; "Exhausted" is no longer chosen by hand and is withdrawn when the block is re-embedded; a used-up or ordered piece cannot be removed from the map; referral end states keep the item's type.
- **FormedrixAI/partners:** Built in the demo build and checked with automated tests. Questions for Pete: "Approve Destructive Cutting" is still offered on a Block exhausted flag (it uses what tissue is left; the spec says no override creates tissue); each waiting slide is flagged one at a time; the megablock cassette template (ZPL-CASSETTE-MEGA-V1) is named in the block-type profile but the cassette print job does not send it yet. Not built: Add-On orders on a matrix block or one of its parts; the exhausted warning in Batch Management's scan; a screen to mark staining racks as large-format (racks are not registered through a screen; only stainers and scanners carry the flag); the cassette template for a megablock is named in the profile but the cassette label path does not yet send it; the large-format label templates must be created on the printers; matrix blocks show their parts as pieces but a matrix part cannot be marked used up. The block-type settings (mold, slide size, templates) are fixed in the code, not yet configurable. Non-English text is machine translated.
- **Standards:** supports traceability of each tissue piece (for example ISO 15189, CAP specimen identification). No certification claimed.

## Batch 731: Exhausted blocks (decided rule), item end states and lineage

- **Practitioner:** On Add-On Orders an exhausted block can now be ticked. The screen says what remains (unstained slides already cut, the part's other blocks) and, for each line, what you can do: put a stain, IHC or molecular test on an unstained slide already cut; ask for the remnant or wet tissue to be re-embedded (the order waits); or order from another block of the same part. A recut or levels cannot use an existing slide. Give a reason and submit; it is audited. When the Microtome flags a block exhausted, the pathologist can answer with the same choices. The Embedding, Microtome and Slide Distribution screens warn when the block is exhausted and say what remains; at Embedding, **Re-embedded: release the orders** sends the waiting orders to the Microtome. The Microtome (for the block) and Slide Distribution (for the slide) have **End state**: exhausted, returned to storage, archived, sent out, consumed for testing or discarded, with a reason. The scan history shows which block a slide came from.
- **System administrator:** New permissions: `material:end-state:record` (roles with the Microtome or Slide Distribution screen), `material:reembed:request` (Pathologist, Fellow, Resident), `material:reembed:complete` (roles with the Embedding screen). Configuration → System → Reporting feed (Power BI): one more dataset, item end states, and a parent item on station scans. Nothing else to set up. End states are also recorded automatically on disposal by scan (discarded), when a referral batch completes (sent out) and when a pathologist confirms a block exhausted (exhausted).
- **Marketing:** Pathologists never hit a dead end on an exhausted block: PathScribe shows what tissue remains and offers the safe alternatives, while every item's final disposition is tracked with a reason, a person and a time.
- **FormedrixAI/partners:** Built in the demo build and checked with automated tests, not yet on a live screen recording. The per-piece check for multi-piece blocks waits for the piece map (Batch 732). At the Microtome flag, "use an unstained slide" is offered for any flagged order except levels at a depth: the order record does not say whether it was a recut, so the pathologist decides. A control slide is not added automatically for a stain put on a slide already cut. Re-embedding a matrix block is not supported. End states made automatically by the system are written by the browser in the demo; the API server must write them inside the action (CASE_WORKFLOWS_API.md). Non-English text is machine translated.
- **Standards:** supports material traceability and disposition records (for example ISO 15189, CAP specimen retention checklist items). No certification claimed.

## Batch 730: Lab Workflow Reports, the reporting feed for Power BI, print labels at, temperature checks

- **Practitioner:** Batch Management has a **Workflow Reports** tile: turnaround by step (accession to gross, embed, cut, stain, distribute, sign-out) with median, 90th percentile and how many were late against your targets; volume per station per day or week; STAT performance; add-on orders and how long they took to cut; recut and rework rates; reconciliation exceptions; and how long Decal and Staining batches took against target. Pick the dates; export any report as CSV. On the Add-On Order page, **Print labels at** can send the new slides' labels to another station's printer when you place the order; the microtome then records the cut without printing them again. The Equipment Log has a **Temperature check**: enter the reading and the outcome follows the device's range.
- **System administrator:** Permissions: `report:<report>:view` and `report:<report>:export` for each of the seven reports (granted with Batch Management; the turnaround, volume and STAT reports also need `station:scan-history:view`). "Print labels at" needs `print:job:redirect` and a station with a slide printer. Configuration → System → Equipment: **Lowest / Highest temperature (°C)** per device. Configuration → System → Reporting feed (Power BI): three more datasets (station scans, carriers and batches, add-on orders). The Power BI kit is in `docs/product/powerbi/`.
- **Marketing:** Lab managers see last month's turnaround by step, bench volume and rework in a few clicks, without a report writer, and Power BI users get the same data through a governed, de-identified feed.
- **FormedrixAI/partners:** The reports are built in the demo build from the demo's scans and cases. The Power BI queries and measures are written but have not been run against real data, and no .pbit template exists (it needs Power BI Desktop): someone must build and test it. The BI feed server and scheduled e-mail delivery of these reports are not built. Steps from embedding on count only work scanned at a station. "Print labels at" prints in the browser through the station's printer profile, like every bench label. Non-English text is machine translated.
- **Standards:** supports turnaround monitoring and equipment temperature records (for example ISO 15189). No certification claimed.

## Batch 729: Matrix blocks at every bench, scan history at the station, and work that has stopped

- **Practitioner:** The Embedding Station now takes matrix blocks (one cassette shared by several specimens, for example M1) like any other block: count the pieces, pick the mold, orient, flag a problem, reprint the label. Slide Distribution takes them too. On the Grossing, Embedding, Microtome and Slide Distribution screens, **Show scan history** lists every station scan of the case's cassettes, blocks and slides: when, which item, where, who and what happened. At Grossing, scanning a cassette from another case shows an orange warning; **Set it aside** or **Continue anyway** (recorded). At the Microtome a line tells you when other blocks of the same case are still waiting to be cut. Printing a slide label and confirming a block are now recorded as steps at your station.
- **System administrator:** Display Profiles: **Not-scanned warning (minutes)** for the Stations view (30 to 10080, blank means 240). The Stations view now lists items not scanned recently and counts items moving without a carrier. Scan history is shown only to roles with `station:scan-history:view` (granted in Batch 726). No new permission.
- **Marketing:** Every cassette, block and slide can be followed bench by bench, including shared matrix blocks, and the wall flags anything that has gone quiet before it is lost.
- **FormedrixAI/partners:** Done in the demo build. Not yet: a Case History screen away from the benches (the trail is on the bench screens); wrong-item checks at Embedding and the Microtome beyond the carrier checks of Batch 726 (a block scanned at the wrong step is not flagged on its own); a warning for an unfinished step other than sectioning; the cell-block and decant types at Embedding and Slide Distribution. The "not scanned" list depends on people scanning at every station; items never scanned at a station do not appear. Non-English text is machine translated.
- **Standards:** supports chain-of-custody and traceability requirements (for example ISO 15189). No certification claimed.

## Batch 728: Research cases kept out of clinical work, and a purpose for every carrier

- **Practitioner:** Research specimens accessioned under the lab's research patient (or with the lab's research accession prefix) no longer appear on your worklist or in your turnaround and volume figures; find them with Case Search. Nothing about them goes to the EHR, by fax, to billing or to a registry. When you make a carrier in **New Container**, the step is no longer pre-set to Decal: it comes from your station, or you choose it. A new **Purpose** field says what the carrier is for (Routine unless you pick Research, Teaching, Clinical Trial, Biobank, QA / Validation or one your lab added), and a carrier that is not Routine shows its purpose on its detail page.
- **System administrator:** Configuration → System → **Research Cases & Batch Purposes**. Research cases: switch on or off; accession prefixes (default `RES`); research patient MRN prefixes (default `RSCH-`); the no-charge financial class (default "Research - No Charge"); days to keep material after accessioning (default 365). Batch purposes: switch the six built-in purposes on or off (Routine stays on), add your own. Display Profiles: **Show carriers for these purposes** (none ticked shows all). Permissions: `config:research-cases:manage`, `config:batch-purposes:manage` (Admin and Lab Director by default). Register the research patient with an MRN that starts with the prefix.
- **Marketing:** Research, teaching and trial work runs through the same lab with the same tracking, without touching clinical worklists, turnaround figures, the EHR or billing.
- **FormedrixAI/partners:** Done in the demo build. It is a workaround: research items are still accessioned cases against a dummy patient; unaccessioned de-identified items are a later phase. Not yet: the report screen does not say "research, not a clinical report" (a research case can still be signed out, but nothing is sent); no research worklist; quality figures outside the Contribution dashboards and the server's BI feed are not yet filtered (the rule is written into `BI_FEED_API.md`); no research case is seeded in the demo. Non-English text is machine translated.
- **Standards:** none claimed. It supports data minimisation (HIPAA, GDPR): nothing about a research case leaves PathScribe.

## Batch 727: Add-ons on the wall display, one time limit per lab, and a Stations view

- **Practitioner:** The Embedding & Microtome wall view now shows add-on orders waiting to be cut, next to the batches, even when they are in no carrier. Each add-on row has an **ADD-ON** tag (beside STAT when it is STAT), says what kind of work it is (recut / levels, special stain, IHC, sections for send-out or molecular), and counts down to the lab's time limit. It turns amber shortly before the limit and red after it. A STAT add-on, or one past the limit, is pinned to the top. An **Add-Ons waiting** tile keeps the total in view. A new **Stations** view shows, step by step, how many carriers and items are waiting and how long the oldest has waited, plus STAT and late work, missing or unexpected items, cold-chain excursions and failed instrument runs. Everything on the wall now appears in the display's language.
- **System administrator:** Configuration → System → Display Profiles: **Time limit X (minutes)** (15 to 1440; blank means 120), one per profile, shown when Embedding & Microtome or Stations is ticked; and the new **Stations** view. No new permission.
- **Marketing:** Add-on work no longer disappears between the pathologist's order and the microtome: it is on the wall, timed, and pushed to the top when it is late or urgent.
- **FormedrixAI/partners:** Done in the demo build against demo data held in the browser (the wall displays' existing limit). The time limit is one per display profile, not per order type. The Stations view counts open carriers and add-ons; items moving with no carrier and no add-on (for example a single block carried by hand) are not counted on the wall until the station scan events feed it (the reports in Batch 730 do use them). Non-English text is machine translated.
- **Standards:** none claimed.

## Batch 726: Carriers at the workstations, and every station scan recorded

- **Practitioner:** The Embedding, Microtome and Slide Distribution screens now have a **Carriers at this station** card. It lists the open baskets, racks and trays for your station. Scan a carrier's barcode (CONT- or RACK-) and it opens right there. A carrier coming from the step before (a processor basket arriving at Embedding, say) moves to your step when you scan it, as the same carrier. Then scan each item as you take it out: the card ticks it off and tells you what is still to scan, and **Finish unloading** closes the carrier. If you scan something that is not in the carrier, or a carrier meant for another step, the screen stops and asks; **Continue anyway** carries on and records it. A closed or unknown carrier is said plainly. On the Microtome, scan a staining rack to make it your output tray and add the printed slides to it. Every scan is now recorded with your station, so the lab can see where each cassette and slide has been. If your computer has no station set, the card asks you to choose one; until then nothing is recorded. In Batch Management a carrier now keeps its barcode from step to step, can be sealed (nothing added or removed), and shows the steps it has been through; the Decal **Transfer to Processing** moves the same batch on instead of making a new one. On the Grossing bench, "Send to decal" became **Load into carrier** (decal vessels and processor baskets).
- **System administrator:** Set a scan station on every computer at a bench (Configuration → System → Scan Stations, or the station picker on the screen). The workflow stage of a station decides which carriers its screen shows; new stages: Decal / Special Processing, Slide Distribution, Checkout, External Referral and the three cytology steps. Four new permissions (Role Dictionary): `batch:manifest:seal`, `batch:carrier:advance`, `station:scan-mismatch:continue`, `station:scan-history:view`, given to every role with Batch Management or a bench workstation (Pathologists Assistant, Histotechnologist, Cytotechnologist, Molecular Technologist). The Decal transfer now needs `batch:carrier:advance`. The barcode prefixes come from Print Settings.
- **Marketing:** PathScribe now tracks every cassette, block and slide at every bench, with or without a carrier. Scan the basket and the whole load arrives at the bench in one step; scan each piece out and nothing goes missing without the system noticing. Wrong items and wrong carriers are caught at the bench, not discovered later.
- **FormedrixAI/partners:** Done in the demo build, against the demo services; the API server side is a written contract (`STATION_TRACKING_API.md`, `CASE_WORKFLOWS_API.md`), not built. The station scan log keeps the last 20,000 events in the browser. Not yet built from the spec, and scheduled: add-ons and the live set on the wall displays (727), batch purpose and the research workaround (728), the Case History scan trail, wrong-item checks at Grossing, lost-work warnings, printed and completed events, matrix blocks at Embedding (729), reports, reporting feed and Power BI kit (730), lineage, end states and the exhausted-block rule (731), block types and piece maps (732). Accessioning has no carriers, so its station reads none. The Embedding and Microtome screens still accept an item without a carrier (the carrier is optional, Decision 8). The screenshots of the three workstations and Batch Management in the User Guide predate the card. Non-English text is machine translated. The training videos for these screens must be re-recorded after testing.
- **Standards:** supports CAP chain-of-custody and specimen-tracking expectations and ISO 15189 traceability (item-level records with who, where and when); no certification claimed.

## Batch 725: The in-app User Guide scrolls

- **Practitioner:** Long User Guide pages can now be scrolled. Before, a page that was longer than the screen was cut off at the bottom and there was no way to read the rest. Now the article scrolls on the right and the contents list scrolls on its own on the left, so the contents stay in view while you read. On a narrow window the whole page scrolls.
- **System administrator:** Nothing to set up; no permission, setting or text, so no language files changed. The Administrator Guide opens in its own window and was not affected.
- **Marketing:** The built-in User Guide reads like a normal help site: the contents stay put while you read.
- **FormedrixAI/partners:** A real defect since the guide was added (Batch 657): the app deliberately never scrolls the whole page, and the guide page had no scroll area of its own. Fixed with styles only (`src/pages/Help/userGuide.css`), with a test (`userGuide.scroll.test.ts`). Checked in a real browser on a test page that copies the app's layout (old styles: no scrolling at all; new: scrolls); not yet checked in the signed-in app itself. Not built: a "back to top" button or a table of contents inside each article.
- **Standards:** supports WCAG 2.1 SC 1.4.10 (Reflow) in that the narrow layout scrolls the one page.

## Batch 724: "Microtome", not "Microtomy", in the English text

- **Practitioner:** The microtome bench now reads **Microtome** everywhere you see it: the Microtome Workspace tile under Pathology Workspace, the Microtome Workstation screen, the workflow stage "Microtome / Sectioning" and the voice and hotkey labels. Nothing about how the bench works changed, and the screen opens from the same place with the same permission.
- **System administrator:** Wording only. No new permission, setting or screen. The address of the screen (`/workstations/microtomy`), the permission (`screen:microtomy:open`), the User Guide and Admin Guide links, and the stored workflow-stage value "Microtomy / Sectioning" in saved stations, batches and cases are unchanged, so nothing needs migrating and existing records still match. Only the English label is new. Stored values are shown through a label, so a batch saved at the "Microtomy / Sectioning" step now displays as "Microtome / Sectioning".
- **Marketing:** The product now uses the laboratory's own word for the bench: Microtome.
- **FormedrixAI/partners:** Done in the demo build, English wording only. Not done: French, German, Dutch, Korean and Spanish keep their own words (microtomie, Mikrotomie, 마이크로톰, microtomía) and need a native-speaker check; the screenshots of the Microtome screen and Pathology Workspace in the User Guide still show the old wording until they are re-taken; the Korean Admin Guide glossary still carries the English word "microtomy" in brackets; the translation-review glossary file still lists "Microtomy". The audit event for engraved slides now reads "Slides Engraved at Microtome"; earlier audit entries keep the old text, because audit records are not rewritten.
- **Standards:** none claimed.

## Batch 723: Home screenshot in the User Guide replaced

- **Practitioner:** The picture of the Home page in the User Guide is now a current capture. It shows the article line, and the tile descriptions match the ones the guide lists.
- **System administrator:** Nothing to set up; no permission, setting or text changed.
- **Marketing:** The guide's Home page now looks like the product does today.
- **FormedrixAI/partners:** Documentation only: `capture:home-overview` replaced by a capture from Pete's computer, scaled to 1,600 px wide. The capture shows Pete's demo account name (staff names are not patient PHI) and no patient data. No app code changed.
- **Standards:** none claimed.

## Batch 722: The Home article line and paste box are in the User Guide, with a picture

- **Practitioner:** The Home page of the User Guide now shows the "From PubMed" article line under your welcome greeting, with a picture, and explains the small box under it: paste a PubMed number or link, press Enter, and that article is shown instead. It also says plainly what the line is (the newest PubMed match for pathology and your subspecialty, not reviewed by your laboratory, so reading and not guidance) and why it can be missing.
- **System administrator:** Nothing to set up and no new permission, setting or text, so no language files changed. The line is switched on or off in Configuration > System > Administration & Compliance > Research Feed ("Show the feed on the dashboard"; opening it needs the permission `config:research-feed:manage`). The same screen shows the last attempt and last successful retrieval, so it is the first place to look when the line is missing. PathScribe's browser must be able to reach `eutils.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov`; a network that blocks it hides the line without an error message.
- **Marketing:** Home keeps clinicians current: a fresh pathology article in their subspecialty greets them each day, and they can swap in a paper a colleague recommended in one paste.
- **FormedrixAI/partners:** Documentation only (guide text, one new screenshot, `capture:home-pubmed`, cropped from a capture on Pete's computer; no app code changed). The feed is automatic: no editorial review, and when PubMed cannot be reached it disappears silently. The training videos recorded in the cloud workspace do not show it for that reason (the workspace cannot reach PubMed); this guide picture is what shows it. Not built: a message on Home saying why the line is missing.
- **Standards:** none claimed.

## Batch 721: Accession screen in two columns on a wide window

- **Practitioner:** On a wide window (about 1,480 pixels or more, so a full-HD monitor) the Accession screen now shows **Case & Patient** on the left and the **Specimens** on the right, both at once. You no longer click **Next: Specimens** and **Back** to move between them, and you can see the patient and the specimen together while you type. For a cytology specimen the Cytology card (last menstrual period, HPV result and the rest) sits under the specimens, next to the specimen that makes it relevant. On a narrower window nothing changes: the same two tabs, one at a time. For an Outside / Contract case, the **Outside Patient Data** button sits beside Specimens and swaps the right column while you use it.
- **System administrator:** Nothing to set up. There is no new permission, setting or text, so no language files changed. The window width that switches the layout is fixed at 1,480 CSS pixels in `services/accessioning/resolveAccessionPanels.ts` (`ACCESSION_TWO_COLUMN_MIN_WIDTH_PX`). Browser zoom changes the width the page sees: at 100% a 1,920-pixel monitor gets two columns, and zooming in past about 130% returns to tabs. User Guide, Accession page, edited: Screen Tour, Toolbar, Hotkeys and Standard workflow. The Accession screenshots in that page need re-taking on a wide window.
- **Marketing:** Accessioning now fits the way a wide monitor is used: the patient on one side, the specimens on the other, and one fewer click on every case.
- **FormedrixAI/partners:** Done in the demo build, layout only: the same fields, rules and buttons, rearranged. Not built: a user setting to force tabs or columns, a different breakpoint per site, and a column layout for the other tall screens (Grossing, Report). Alt+. and Alt+, and Alt+Enter switch tabs, so on a wide window they change nothing you can see. The tall single column was also the reason the training videos had to shrink this screen to 50%; with columns the whole screen, Cytology card included, fits at about 67% zoom. Still too small to read comfortably in a window, so the Accession videos zoom in on each column.
- **Standards:** supports WCAG 1.4.10 (Reflow) in that narrow windows and high zoom keep the single-column layout.

## Batch 720: Grossing tile and bench tools, required marks, labels print on save

- **Practitioner:** Margin Exception Review is now a tile under Pathology Workspace, beside Grossing and Add-On Orders, so pathologists no longer see a Quality & Compliance tile on Home that held only that one screen (roles with other quality screens keep the hub). On the Worklist the "Outreach" tile is now "Orchestration Cases" (tooltips and the Back-to label follow). Pathology assistants and grossing pathologists now have a **Grossing** tile under Pathology Workspace. It lists the cases waiting to be grossed, urgent first. The Grossing screen has a **Bench tools** card: print cassettes, take a gross photo (foot pedal 1), print with foot pedal 2, send a block to a decalcification batch, release cassettes through the triage checklist, and open the Add-On Orders builder. Alt+Shift+1 to 4 and the voice phrases do the same. Physicians still open the screen from the report's Material tab. On Accession, required fields now carry a red asterisk, and labels print by themselves when you save (the Print Labels button stays for reprints). If the automatic template check is unavailable, a plain note says each specimen kept its default template.
- **System administrator:** new permission `screen:grossing:open` (Role Dictionary > Screens), seeded to Pathologist, Fellow, Resident and Pathologists Assistant; the PA also now gets the Add-On Orders screen. The four grossing actions are granted to the same roles. Configuration > Print Settings has a new checkbox, "Print labels when a case is accessioned" (on by default, can be overridden per facility). Which fields show an asterisk follows Field Requirements.
- **Marketing:** Grossing now has its own front door and a bench-side toolbar, hands-free where the lab wants it: foot pedals, voice and hotkeys, with labels printed the moment a case is accessioned.
- **FormedrixAI/partners:** Done in the demo build. Not built: choosing which printer or station add-on labels print at when the order is placed (they print at the microtome bench after the block is scanned), a grouped "Referred from Outside Lab" heading on Accession (the two boxes are now relabelled Outside Lab Name and Outside Lab Specimen / Accession ID instead). The browser-print window now opens from the click, before the save finishes, so pop-up blockers do not stop it; if the save fails the window closes. Non-English strings are machine translated.

## Batch 669h: Control Rules reachable, External Consult masked by default, honest footer status

- Practitioner: Control Rules now opens for the people who set and review the rules (Lab Director, Administrator, Quality Reviewer) as well as the molecular technologists, without anyone needing the whole Molecular screen. Technologists still only read; changes need the manage permission.
- System administrator: new permission "View molecular assay control rules" (`config:molecular-control-rules:view`), Role Dictionary > Administration. Seeded to Administrator, Laboratory Director, Quality Reviewer, Molecular Technologist; "Manage ..." now needs it and stays with Administrator and Laboratory Director. A role with either opens the page by address or from the Molecular screen's button; the page's list is refused without it. Plates and racks are still checked against the rules for everyone who works them.
- Marketing: The people accountable for molecular quality can now open and audit the assay control rules directly, with read and change rights kept separate.
- FormedrixAI/partners: Fixes a real dead end (neither Admin nor Lab Director could reach the page). The rules are still enforced on plates regardless of who may view them. Not done: no dedicated molecular supervisor role exists, so nobody beyond the four roles above has it until an administrator assigns it; the Superadmin holds it like every capability.

- **Practitioner:** The page an outside consultant opens no longer shows the patient's name and birth date. It shows initials (J*** S****) and the birth year only (**/**/1980), plus the accession, specimens and slides they need. If you really need the consultant to see who the patient is, tick "Show the patient's full name and date of birth to the consultant" when you issue the link (off by default; Pathologists and Lab Directors can).
- **System administrator:** New capability `case:consult-link:reveal-identity` (Pathologist, Lab Director; grant to other roles in Configuration). The tick box is greyed out without it. The audit entry for the new link says whether the opt-in was used; each view says which version was shown. Links cannot be changed afterwards: revoke and reissue. Existing links are masked. Locale keys added in six languages.
- **Marketing:** Outside second opinions with privacy built in: consultants see what they need to give an opinion, and identity is shared only when a pathologist deliberately chooses it, with a permanent audit record.
- **FormedrixAI/partners (plain reality):** Masking is done before the data leaves the service, not by hiding it on screen, but the link is still a bearer secret: anyone holding it can open it, the accession is still shown, and name length is visible in the mask. There is no passcode/e-mail identity check (Planned; needs the API server and a mail sender), no single-use link, and in this build links still open only in the issuing browser, so nothing here is production-ready until the API server (CONSULT_ACCESS_API.md CA-25 to CA-27) is built.

- **Practitioner:** The bottom of Home no longer says "HIPAA Compliant" and no longer says "SYSTEMS OPERATIONAL" as fixed words. It shows a coloured dot and the real state: Checking, Operational, Degraded, Unavailable, or Status unavailable when the check could not be made. It looks again when you come back to the tab after five minutes. The secure-email sent screen no longer says "Encrypted in transit . Audit logged".
- **System administrator:** The status comes from `GET /api/health/health-status/check`, which reports database, messaging, queues and interface engine; the server decides the overall state. Until the server has the route the footer shows "Status unavailable". Locale keys: added `home.status.*`, changed `home.footerCopyright`, removed `home.systemsOperational` and `appShell.secureEmailModal.encryptedNotice`.
- **Marketing:** The Home screen shows live system health from the server, checked when you open it and when you return to it, and it only says what it has checked.
- **FormedrixAI/partners (plain reality):** The server route does not exist yet (contract written, HS-1 to HS-5); in the demo the status only reflects whether the browser is online and is labelled simulated. No HIPAA or CAP compliance statement is printed anywhere on these screens. The secure-email send is still simulated. The CAP wording is "structured on", not certified.

## Batch 716: realistic cytology demo data

**For the practitioner (User Guide).** The demo now has realistic cytology specimens: Pap (ThinPrep and SurePath), Pap with HPV, HPV-only swab, urine, pleural, peritoneal, CSF, bronchial and sputum, and thyroid, lymph node, breast and salivary FNAs. Choose one at Accession and the cytology history card appears, the case takes its own number series (GYN, NG or S), and ready-made orders can be imported.

**For the system administrator (Admin Guide).** A Configuration Full Reset loads the new entries, a GYN Cytology department and its case mask, four cytology protocols (Pap with HPV co-test, HPV-only swab, fluid cell block, FNA cell block), pending orders ord-030 to ord-037, and non-GYN routing set to the Cytology worklist. Every surgical entry is now linked to the Surgical Tissue department, so specimens of different case types are blocked from one accession. All of it can be changed in Configuration. See DEMO_GUIDE.md, Cytology demo data.

**For marketing (positive).** See a Pap with HPV, a thyroid FNA and a pleural fluid move through Accession, each in its own accession series, with the HPV order queued and the cases waiting on the Cytology Worklist.

**For FormedrixAI and partners (the plain reality).** This is demo seed data, not a new feature. Slides and smears are not generated, only decants, and every protocol decant is typed cell block. The HPV order is sent but results and reflex are not automated, and the protocol setting that sends it has no screen. Several FNA and fluid types have no default template. An existing browser needs one Full Reset to load the data.

## Batch 715: letters or numbers for specimens, blocks and slides

**For the practitioner (User Guide).** Your laboratory chooses whether specimens, blocks and slides are labelled with letters or numbers, so cassette and slide labels match what your histology staff already write. Add Block in Grossing and on the report screen follows the choice.

**For the system administrator (Admin Guide).** Configuration > System > Facility Configuration: three selectors (specimen, block, slide) at enterprise level, with an optional override for each performing facility and an inherit choice, and a live example. The old combined option stays as a preset. Each edit is audited. It applies to new cases; existing and printed labels are not changed.

**For marketing (positive).** Labels follow your lab's own convention, including mixed styles such as 1A, 1B or A1, A2.

**For FormedrixAI and partners (the plain reality).** There is no Relabel existing, by design, so printed and signed labels are never silently changed. The slide level code (L1, L2) stays in slide IDs and barcodes, and some on-screen level columns still show it. Choosing the same type for specimens and blocks only warns. Adding a facility is not audited. Five languages are machine-translated and need review.

## Batch 714: Clinical Indication shows where it came from

**For the practitioner (User Guide).** When you import an order, the Clinical Indication fills in with the ordering clinician's reason, and a note says which order field it came from. Read it and edit it if needed. If you change it, the note says it was edited and shows the original. Both are kept, and the text feeds the Clinical-Pathologic Mismatch Check.

**For the system administrator (Admin Guide).** No setting. The order source must supply clinicalIndication and clinicalIndicationSource; the mapping (HL7 OBR-31, ORC-16, DG1, NTE; FHIR ServiceRequest reasonCode and reasonReference) is the contract in ACCESSIONING_API.md section 1a. The case keeps orderClinicalIndication and its field beside the edited text.

**For marketing (positive).** The reason the surgeon gave stays attached to the case with its origin shown, and any edit is traceable.
